I mean, I’ve been stressed and kind of discouraged about work, but I don’t know if that counts as “down.” Maybe sometimes, but I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends and family, about as much as you always have?\n\nPatient: Not really, I guess. I mean, I still try to do some of those things, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped. But my little printer for miniatures has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months. And even things like yoga or going for a walk in the woods… they kind of feel like another task I’m supposed to do, not something I actually want to do. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things that used to bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but things I used to actually look forward to just don’t feel the same. Like, my miniatures printer has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months, and even yoga or walking in the woods feels more like something I’m supposed to check off than something I really want to do. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, my clothes seem to fit about the same, so I don’t think there’s been anything significant. I don’t weigh myself very regularly, though, so… I might not be the best judge.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know, maybe a little, but I haven’t really been tracking it closely. I know that sounds small, but it’s been getting in the way, and I thought I should probably talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this difficulty with thinking clearly at work?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a little over a year, I think. It started after I got moved into the prototype-testing role — about a year and a month ago. I mean, it wasn’t all at once or anything, but that’s when I started noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you started experiencing these difficulties with thinking clearly at work?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d say sad most of the time, exactly. I mean, I’ve been stressed and kind of discouraged about work, but I don’t know if that counts as “down.” Maybe sometimes, but I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends and family, about as much as you always have?\n\nPatient: Not really, I guess. I mean, I still try to do some of those things, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped. But my little printer for miniatures has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months. And even things like yoga or going for a walk in the woods… they kind of feel like another task I’m supposed to do, not something I actually want to do. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things that used to bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but things I used to actually look forward to just don’t feel the same. Like, my miniatures printer has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months, and even yoga or walking in the woods feels more like something I’m supposed to check off than something I really want to do. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, my clothes seem to fit about the same, so I don’t think there’s been anything significant. I don’t weigh myself very regularly, though, so… I might not be the best judge.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know, maybe a little, but I haven’t really been tracking it closely. Some nights are better than others. I guess I wouldn’t want to call it a major problem unless that’s what you mean? I can pay more attention to it if that would help.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I get tired like anyone does, especially after work, but I haven’t really been keeping track of whether it’s more than usual. Maybe a bit? I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, a little. I mean, maybe more than a little, I guess. I keep thinking about small mistakes I’ve made at the lab, things that probably other people would just fix and move on from, but I replay them over and over.\n\nAnd then it turns into this feeling like I’m not just bad at the job, but… bad as a person. Like if something happens because of it, I deserve it. I know that probably sounds dramatic, sorry. I’m trying to answer honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say hopeless, exactly. I mean, I have days where it’s hard to picture things getting much better, but maybe everyone feels that way sometimes. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I guess I’m not really sure how to answer that correctly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, sometimes. I don’t want to sound like I’m being overly dramatic, but I keep replaying these tiny mistakes from the lab, and it turns into me thinking I’m not just bad at the job, I’m just… bad. Like as a person. And then I feel like whatever consequences come from it, I probably deserve them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy doing things that normally bring you pleasure, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed some. I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve stopped everything completely, but things I usually like just feel kind of… flat, I guess.\n\nLike my printer for miniatures has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months, and I keep meaning to go back to it, but I just don’t. I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s kind of become this loop where I replay small mistakes from the lab and then it turns into, like, “I’m not just bad at this job, I’m bad as a person.” And then I feel like I deserve whatever happens because of it. Is that… the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many days a week would you say you've been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure I’ve been keeping track that clearly. It’s not like I mark it on a calendar or anything.\n\nMaybe… several days? I don’t want to guess wrong. Some weeks it feels like more than others, but I’m not sure I could give you an exact number. Is it okay if I say it feels frequent, but I don’t know the exact days?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It started after I got moved into the prototype-testing role — about a year and a month ago. I mean, it wasn’t all at once or anything, but that’s when I started noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you started experiencing these difficulties with thinking clearly at work?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d say sad most of the time, exactly. I mean, I’ve been stressed and kind of discouraged about work, but I don’t know if that counts as “down.” Maybe sometimes, but I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends and family, about as much as you always have?\n\nPatient: Not really, I guess. I mean, I still try to do some of those things, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped. But my little printer for miniatures has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months. And even things like yoga or going for a walk in the woods… they kind of feel like another task I’m supposed to do, not something I actually want to do. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things that used to bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but things I used to actually look forward to just don’t feel the same. Like, my miniatures printer has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months, and even yoga or walking in the woods feels more like something I’m supposed to check off than something I really want to do. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, my clothes seem to fit about the same, so I don’t think there’s been anything significant. I don’t weigh myself very regularly, though, so… I might not be the best judge.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know, maybe a little, but I haven’t really been tracking it closely. Some nights are better than others. I guess I wouldn’t want to call it a major problem unless that’s what you mean? I can pay more attention to it if that would help.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I get tired like anyone does, especially after work, but I haven’t really been keeping track of whether it’s more than usual. Maybe a bit? I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, a little. I mean, maybe more than a little, I guess. I keep thinking about small mistakes I’ve made at the lab, things that probably other people would just fix and move on from, but I replay them over and over.\n\nAnd then it turns into this feeling like I’m not just bad at the job, but… bad as a person. Like if something happens because of it, I deserve it. I know that probably sounds dramatic, sorry. I’m trying to answer honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say hopeless, exactly. I mean, I have days where it’s hard to picture things getting much better, but maybe everyone feels that way sometimes. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I guess I’m not really sure how to answer that correctly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, sometimes. I don’t want to sound like I’m being overly dramatic, but I keep replaying these tiny mistakes from the lab, and it turns into me thinking I’m not just bad at the job, I’m just… bad. Like as a person. And then I feel like whatever consequences come from it, I probably deserve them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy doing things that normally bring you pleasure, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed some. I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve stopped everything completely, but things I usually like just feel kind of… flat, I guess.\n\nLike my printer for miniatures has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months, and I keep meaning to go back to it, but I just don’t. And even yoga or walking in the woods—things that usually help—feel more like another task I’m supposed to do than something I actually want to do. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of worthlessness and guilt nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Nearly every day… I think so, yes. I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s kind of become this loop where I replay small mistakes from the lab and then it turns into, like, “I’m not just bad at this job, I’m bad as a person.” And then I feel like I deserve whatever happens because of it. Is that… the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many days a week would you say you've been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure I’ve been keeping track that clearly. It’s not like I mark it on a calendar or anything.\n\nMaybe… several days? I don’t want to guess wrong. Some weeks it feels like more than others, but I’m not sure I could give you an exact number. Is it okay if I say it feels frequent, but I don’t know the exact days?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your feelings of depression are mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I’d probably say… moderate? I mean, I can still get through some things, so I don’t want to call it severe if that sounds like I’m completely unable to function. But it’s more than mild, I think. It’s there most of the time and it takes effort to push through. Does moderate sound right for that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I mean… there are things I used to enjoy, but I don’t really look forward to them the same way. My miniature printer has just been sitting there with the same half-finished figure for months, which is kind of embarrassing.\n\nEven things that should be easy, like yoga or going for a walk in the woods, feel more like another task I’m supposed to do than something I actually want to do. I can still try sometimes, but it doesn’t feel… enjoyable exactly. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I want to hurt myself exactly. Or I don’t want to say it that way if that sounds too dramatic.\n\nBut… on my commute sometimes I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t arrive anywhere. And a couple times the thought of steering off the road has come up, which scares me enough that I grip the wheel really hard or pull over. I’m not trying to do anything, but… it does happen. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I’ve been stressed and kind of discouraged about work, but I don’t know if that counts as “down.” Maybe sometimes, but I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends and family, about as much as you always have?\n\nPatient: Not really, I guess. I mean, I still try to do some of those things, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped. But my little printer for miniatures has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months. And even things like yoga or going for a walk in the woods… they kind of feel like another task I’m supposed to do, not something I actually want to do. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things that used to bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but things I used to actually look forward to just don’t feel the same. Like, my miniatures printer has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months, and even yoga or walking in the woods feels more like something I’m supposed to check off than something I really want to do. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, my clothes seem to fit about the same, so I don’t think there’s been anything significant. I don’t weigh myself very regularly, though, so… I might not be the best judge.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know, maybe a little, but I haven’t really been tracking it closely. Some nights are better than others. I guess I wouldn’t want to call it a major problem unless that’s what you mean? I can pay more attention to it if that would help.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I get tired like anyone does, especially after work, but I haven’t really been keeping track of whether it’s more than usual. Maybe a bit? I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, a little. I mean, maybe more than a little, I guess. I keep thinking about small mistakes I’ve made at the lab, things that probably other people would just fix and move on from, but I replay them over and over.\n\nAnd then it turns into this feeling like I’m not just bad at the job, but… bad as a person. Like if something happens because of it, I deserve it. I know that probably sounds dramatic, sorry. I’m trying to answer honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say hopeless, exactly. I mean, I have days where it’s hard to picture things getting much better, but maybe everyone feels that way sometimes. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I guess I’m not really sure how to answer that correctly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, sometimes. I don’t want to sound like I’m being overly dramatic, but I keep replaying these tiny mistakes from the lab, and it turns into me thinking I’m not just bad at the job, I’m just… bad. Like as a person. And then I feel like whatever consequences come from it, I probably deserve them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy doing things that normally bring you pleasure, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed some. I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve stopped everything completely, but things I usually like just feel kind of… flat, I guess.\n\nLike my printer for miniatures has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months, and I keep meaning to go back to it, but I just don’t. And even yoga or walking in the woods—things that usually help—feel more like another task I’m supposed to do than something I actually want to do. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of worthlessness and guilt nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Nearly every day… I think so, yes. I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s kind of become this loop where I replay small mistakes from the lab and then it turns into, like, “I’m not just bad at this job, I’m bad as a person.” And then I feel like I deserve whatever happens because of it. Is that… the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many days a week would you say you've been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure I’ve been keeping track that clearly. It’s not like I mark it on a calendar or anything.\n\nMaybe… several days? I don’t want to guess wrong. Some weeks it feels like more than others, but I’m not sure I could give you an exact number. Is it okay if I say it feels frequent, but I don’t know the exact days?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your feelings of depression are mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I’d probably say… moderate? I mean, I can still get through some things, so I don’t want to call it severe if that sounds like I’m completely unable to function. But it’s more than mild, I think. It’s there most of the time and it takes effort to push through. Does moderate sound right for that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I mean… there are things I used to enjoy, but I don’t really look forward to them the same way. My miniature printer has just been sitting there with the same half-finished figure for months, which is kind of embarrassing.\n\nEven things that should be easy, like yoga or going for a walk in the woods, feel more like another task I’m supposed to do than something I actually want to do. I can still try sometimes, but it doesn’t feel… enjoyable exactly. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I want to hurt myself exactly. Or I don’t want to say it that way if that sounds too dramatic.\n\nBut… on my commute sometimes I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t arrive anywhere. And a couple times the thought of steering off the road has come up, which scares me enough that I grip the wheel really hard or pull over. I’m not trying to do anything, but… it does happen. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling down or depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It’s been… longer than I’d like to admit, I guess. I think it really started after I was moved into the prototype-testing role at work, and that was about a year and a month ago now.\n\nIt’s not like every single minute is exactly the same, but the overall down feeling has been there since then. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I mean, it wasn’t all at once or anything, but that’s when I started noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you started experiencing these difficulties with thinking clearly at work?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d say sad most of the time, exactly. I mean, I’ve been stressed and kind of discouraged about work, but I don’t know if that counts as “down.” Maybe sometimes, but I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends and family, about as much as you always have?\n\nPatient: Not really, I guess. I mean, I still try to do some of those things, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped. But my little printer for miniatures has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months. And even things like yoga or going for a walk in the woods… they kind of feel like another task I’m supposed to do, not something I actually want to do. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things that used to bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but things I used to actually look forward to just don’t feel the same. Like, my miniatures printer has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months, and even yoga or walking in the woods feels more like something I’m supposed to check off than something I really want to do. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, my clothes seem to fit about the same, so I don’t think there’s been anything significant. I don’t weigh myself very regularly, though, so… I might not be the best judge.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know, maybe a little, but I haven’t really been tracking it closely. Some nights are better than others. I guess I wouldn’t want to call it a major problem unless that’s what you mean? I can pay more attention to it if that would help.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I get tired like anyone does, especially after work, but I haven’t really been keeping track of whether it’s more than usual. Maybe a bit? I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, a little. I mean, maybe more than a little, I guess. I keep thinking about small mistakes I’ve made at the lab, things that probably other people would just fix and move on from, but I replay them over and over.\n\nAnd then it turns into this feeling like I’m not just bad at the job, but… bad as a person. Like if something happens because of it, I deserve it. I know that probably sounds dramatic, sorry. I’m trying to answer honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say hopeless, exactly. I mean, I have days where it’s hard to picture things getting much better, but maybe everyone feels that way sometimes. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I guess I’m not really sure how to answer that correctly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, sometimes. I don’t want to sound like I’m being overly dramatic, but I keep replaying these tiny mistakes from the lab, and it turns into me thinking I’m not just bad at the job, I’m just… bad. Like as a person. And then I feel like whatever consequences come from it, I probably deserve them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy doing things that normally bring you pleasure, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed some. I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve stopped everything completely, but things I usually like just feel kind of… flat, I guess.\n\nLike my printer for miniatures has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months, and I keep meaning to go back to it, but I just don’t. And even yoga or walking in the woods—things that usually help—feel more like another task I’m supposed to do than something I actually want to do. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of worthlessness and guilt nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Nearly every day… I think so, yes. I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s kind of become this loop where I replay small mistakes from the lab and then it turns into, like, “I’m not just bad at this job, I’m bad as a person.” And then I feel like I deserve whatever happens because of it. Is that… the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many days a week would you say you've been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure I’ve been keeping track that clearly. It’s not like I mark it on a calendar or anything.\n\nMaybe… several days? I don’t want to guess wrong. Some weeks it feels like more than others, but I’m not sure I could give you an exact number. Is it okay if I say it feels frequent, but I don’t know the exact days?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your feelings of depression are mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I’d probably say… moderate? I mean, I can still get through some things, so I don’t want to call it severe if that sounds like I’m completely unable to function. But it’s more than mild, I think. It’s there most of the time and it takes effort to push through. Does moderate sound right for that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: I mean… there are things I used to enjoy, but I don’t really look forward to them the same way. My miniature printer has just been sitting there with the same half-finished figure for months, which is kind of embarrassing.\n\nEven things that should be easy, like yoga or going for a walk in the woods, feel more like another task I’m supposed to do than something I actually want to do. I can still try sometimes, but it doesn’t feel… enjoyable exactly. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I want to hurt myself exactly. Or I don’t want to say it that way if that sounds too dramatic.\n\nBut… on my commute sometimes I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t arrive anywhere. And a couple times the thought of steering off the road has come up, which scares me enough that I grip the wheel really hard or pull over. I’m not trying to do anything, but… it does happen. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling down or depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It’s been… longer than I’d like to admit, I guess. I think it really started after I was moved into the prototype-testing role at work, and that was about a year and a month ago now.\n\nIt’s not like every single minute is exactly the same, but the overall down feeling has been there since then. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling down or depressed like this before in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anyone, especially when things were stressful, but I don’t know that it was… like this.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it. I guess this feels more persistent than what I remember before. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
